LARI fibrations

Definition 11.B.2

(Enough
j
-LARI lifts)
.

Let
P:B\to\mathcal{U}
be an isoinner family over a Rezk type
B
, and
j:\Phi\hookrightarrow\Psi
a
j
-LARI lift. Then
P
is said to
have all
or
enough
j
-LARI lifts
if and only if the type

\displaystyle\prod_{\langle u,v,f\rangle:E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}\sum_{g:\left\langle\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\textstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\Psi}}v^{*}P\middle|^{\Phi}_{f}\right\rangle}{\mathrm{isLariCell}}_{j}(g)

(4)

is inhabited.

By
Theorem

11.A.4
, in fact
\sum_{g:\ldots}{\mathrm{isLariCell}}_{j}(g)
is a proposition. Given
\langle u,v,f\rangle
, we denote the arrow part from the center of contraction of this type, occuring in (
4
), as

P_{!}(u,v,f)\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}g_{u,v,f}:\left\langle\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\textstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\Psi}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\Psi}}v^{*}P\middle|^{\Phi}_{f}\right\rangle,

generalizing from cocartesian families.
5
5

5

We could also add the inclusion
j:\Phi\hookrightarrow\Psi
as an annotation, but this is not necessary here since we will only deal with such inclusions one at a time.

Similarly, for
g\equiv P_{!}(u,v,f)
we denote the ensuing “filling” data from
Equation

3
by

\mathrm{fill}_{g}(\alpha,m):\left\langle\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\langle t,s\rangle:\Delta^{1}\times\Psi}}{\textstyle\prod_{\langle t,s\rangle:\Delta^{1}\times\Psi}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\langle t,s\rangle:\Delta^{1}\times\Psi}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\langle t,s\rangle:\Delta^{1}\times\Psi}}P\big(\alpha_{1}(t,s)\big)\middle|^{b_{1}\mathbin{\widehat{\otimes}}j}_{[\langle g,m\rangle,\alpha_{2}]}\right\rangle.\lx@nobreakspace

Theorem 11.B.3

(
j
-LARI families via enough
j
-LARI lifts)
.

Let
B
be a Rezk type,
P:B\to\mathcal{U}
be an isoinner family, and denote by
\pi:E\to B
the associated projection map. Then
P
has enough
j
-LARI lifts if and only if it is a
j
-LARI family,
i.e.
the Leibniz cotensor map
\pi^{\prime}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}i_{0}\mathbin{\widehat{\pitchfork}}\pi:E^{\Psi}\to E^{\Phi}\to_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}
has a left adjoint right inverse:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\Phi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\Psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

Assume
P:B\to\mathcal{U}
is an isoinner family with enough
j
-LARI lifts. The gap map can be taken as the strict projection

\pi^{\prime}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda u,v,f,g.\langle u,v,f\rangle:E^{\Psi}\to E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}.

For the candidate LARI we take the map that produces the
j
-LARI lift,
i.e.

\lx@nobreakspace\chi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda u,v,f.\langle u,v,f,P_{!}(u,v,f)\rangle:E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}\to E^{\Psi}.

This is by definition a (strict) section of
\pi^{\prime}
.

For
\langle u,v,f\rangle:E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}
and
\langle r,w,k,m\rangle:E^{\Psi}
we define the maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{E^{\Psi}}(\chi(u,v,f),\langle r,w,k,m\rangle)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}(\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k\rangle)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k,m\rangle}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k,m\rangle}}

defined by
6
6

6

We decompose morphisms in
E^{\Psi}
as pairs
\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle
where
\alpha
denotes the part in
E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}
and
\beta
is the given
\Psi
-shaped cell in
P
lying over.

F_{\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k,m\rangle}(\alpha,\beta)\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\alpha,\quad G_{\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k,m\rangle}(\gamma)\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\langle\gamma,\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(u,v,f)}(\gamma,m)\rangle\lx@nobreakspace

are quasi-inverse to one another.
7
7

7

For brevity, we shall henceforth leave the fixed parameters
\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k,m\rangle
implicit.

Clearly,
G
is a section of the projection
F
since for a morphism
\gamma
we find

\lx@nobreakspace F(G(\gamma))=F(\gamma,\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(u,v,f)}(\gamma,m))=\gamma.

For a morphism
\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle
in the opposing transposing morphism space we obtain

\lx@nobreakspace G(F(\alpha,\beta))=G(\alpha)=\langle\alpha,\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(u,v,f)}(\alpha,m)\rangle

where we obtain an identification
\beta=\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(u,v,f)}(\alpha,m)
(over
{\mathrm{refl}}_{\alpha}
) because of the universal property
Equation

3
.

This suffices to show that
\chi\dashv\pi^{\prime}
is a LARI adjunction as claimed.

Conversely, suppose
\chi
is a given LARI of
\pi^{\prime}
, w.l.o.g. a strict section. This gives, for any data
\langle u,v,f\rangle:E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}
we obtain a (strictly) commutative triangle:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\Psi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}E^{\Psi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi(u,v,f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u,v,f\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}

Moreover, for all
\langle r,w,k,m\rangle:E^{\Psi}
, the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{E^{\Psi}}(\chi(u,v,f),\langle r,w,k,m\rangle)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hom_{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}(\langle u,v,f\rangle,\langle r,w,k\rangle)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\langle r,w,k,m\rangle}}

defined by

\lx@nobreakspace F(\alpha,\beta)\equiv\alpha

is an equivalence. Finally, contractibility of the fibers amounts to the universal property
Equation

3
, but this precisely means that
\chi(u,v,f)
is a
j
-LARI cell.
∎

3
LARI functors

Let
j:\Phi\hookrightarrow\Psi
be a shape inclusion.

Definition 11.B.4

(
j
-LARI functors)
.

Over Rezk types
A
and
B
, resp., consider
j
-LARI families
Q:A\to\mathcal{U}
and
P:E\to\mathcal{U}
, resp., with

\xi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{A}(Q):F\twoheadrightarrow A,\quad\pi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{E}(P):E\twoheadrightarrow B.

Assume there is a fibered functor from
\xi
to
\pi
given by a commutative square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

This defines a
j
-LARI functor
if and only if the following proposition is satisfied:

\prod_{m:\Psi\to F}{\mathrm{isLariCell}}_{j}^{Q}(m)\to{\mathrm{isLariCell}}_{j}^{P}(\varphi\,m)

Proposition 11.B.5

(Naturality of cocartesian liftings)
.

Let
B
be a Rezk type, and
P:B\to\mathcal{U}
,
Q:C\to\mathcal{U}
, resp. be
j
-LARI families with associated fibrations
\xi:F\twoheadrightarrow A
and
\xi:E\twoheadrightarrow B
, resp. Then the proposition that
\langle\alpha,\varphi\rangle
be a
j
-LARI functor from
\xi
to
\pi
is logically equivalent to
\langle\alpha,\varphi\rangle
commuting with cocartesian lifts:
i.e.
for any
\langle r,w,k,m\rangle:F^{\Phi}\times_{A^{\Phi}}A^{\Psi}
there exists an identification
8
8

8

suppressing the “lower” data which can be taken to consist of identities anyway
of
\Psi
-cells

\lx@nobreakspace\varphi\big(Q_{!}(r,w,k)\big)=P_{!}(\alpha\,r,\alpha\,w,\varphi\,k,\varphi\,d).

Proof.

Since
\varphi
is a
j
-LARI functor by assumption
g\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\varphi\big(Q_{!}(r,w,k)\big)
is a
j
-LARi cell,
i.e.

{g\,{}^{(\langle u,v,f\rangle)}\dashv\pi^{\prime}}
. But also for the cell
g^{\prime}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}P_{!}(\alpha\,r,\alpha\,w,\varphi\,k,\varphi d)
by construction we have

{g^{\prime}\,{}^{(\langle u,v,f\rangle)}\dashv\pi^{\prime}}
, hence there is a homotopy
g=g^{\prime}
, by uniqueness of relative left adjoints.

Conversely, since any
j
-LARI arrow in a family occurs as a
j
-LARI lift (of the data given by projection and restriction), the assumed identifications yield the desired implication.
∎

Theorem 11.B.6

(Chevalley criterion for
j
-LARI functors, cf.
[
BW21
, Theorem 5.3.19]
,
[
RV22
, Theorem 5.3.4]
)
.

Given data as in
Definition

11.B.4
, the following are equivalent:

1.

The fiberwise map
\langle\alpha,\varphi\rangle
is a
j
-LARI functor.

2.

The mate of the induced canonical natural isomorphism is invertible, too:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Delta^{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Phi}\times_{A^{\Phi}}A^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Phi}\times_{A^{\Phi}}A^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Phi}\times_{\alpha^{\Phi}}\alpha^{\Psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Delta^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Phi}\times_{\alpha^{\Phi}}\alpha^{\Psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Delta^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rightsquigarrow}

Proof.

The counit of the adjunction exhibiting
\pi:E\twoheadrightarrow B
as a
j
-LARI fibration, at stage
\langle u,v,f,g\rangle:E^{\Psi}
, can be taken to be

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{u,v,f,g}\equiv\langle\id_{u},\id_{v},\id_{f},\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(u,v,f)}(\id_{u},\id_{v},\id_{f},g)\rangle,

(5)

as one sees by the usual construction from the transposing map, cf. the proof of
Theorem

11.B.3
. Now, from the proof of
[
BW21
, Propositon A.1.2]

9
9

9

cf.
[
BW21
, Theorem 5.3.19]
for the cocartesian case
we see that the pasting cell constructed from the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Phi}\times_{A^{\Phi}}A^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\Phi}\times_{A^{\Phi}}A^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Phi}\times_{B^{\Phi}}B^{\Psi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\Psi}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Phi}\times_{\alpha^{\Phi}}\alpha^{\Psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\Psi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}

is given, at stage
\langle r,w,k,m\rangle:F^{\Psi}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varepsilon}_{r,w,k,m}=\varepsilon_{\alpha\,r,\alpha\,w,\varphi\,k,\varphi(Q_{!}(r,w,k))}

\displaystyle=\langle\id_{\alpha\,r},\id_{\alpha\,w},\id_{\varphi\,k},\mathrm{fill}_{P_{!}(\alpha\,r,\alpha\,w,\alpha\,k)}(\id_{\alpha\,r},\id_{\alpha\,w},\id_{\varphi\,k},\varphi Q_{!}(r,w,k))\rangle.

This collapses to just the comparison map
P_{!}(\alpha\,r,\alpha\,w,\alpha\,k)\to\varphi Q_{!}(r,w,k)
given by filling. Now by
11.B.5
the invertibility of this is equivalent to
\langle\alpha,\varphi\rangle
being a
j
-LARI functor.
∎

Chapter 12
Fibered and sliced constructions

Appendix 12.A
Fibered equivalences

We state some expected and useful closure properties of fibered equivalences.

Lemma 12.A.1

(Right properness)
.

Pullbacks of weak equivalences are weak equivalences again,
i.e.
given a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\times_{A}B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

then, as indicated if the right vertical map is a weak equivalence, then so is the left hand one.

Proof.

Denote by
P:A\to\mathcal{U}
the straightening of
k:B\to A
, so that
B\simeq\sum_{a:A}P\,a
, and
D\simeq\sum_{c:C}P(j\,c)
. The map
k:B\to A
(identified with its fibrant replacement) is a weak equivalence if and only if
\prod_{a:A}\isContr(P\,a)
. This implies
\prod_{c:C}P(j\,c)
which is equivalent to
k^{\prime}=j^{*}k
being a weak equivalence, as desired.
∎

Proposition 12.A.2

(Homotopy invariance of homotopy pullbacks)
.

Given a map between cospans of types

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq~r}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq~p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq~q}

where the vertical arrows are weak euivalences the induced map

B\times_{A}C\to B^{\prime}\times_{A^{\prime}}C^{\prime}

is an equivalence as well.

Proof.

By right properness and
2
-out-of-
3
the mediating map
C\to P\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}C^{\prime}\times_{A^{\prime}}A
is an equivalence as can be seen from the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

This gives the following cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C\times_{A}B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}\times_{A^{\prime}}B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and by the Pullback Lemma we know that the back face is a pullback, too. Then again, by right properness

B\times_{A}C\to B^{\prime}\times_{A^{\prime}}C^{\prime}

is an equivalence.
∎

Proposition 12.A.3
.

Given a fibered equivalence as below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

and a map
k:A\to B
the fibered equivalence
\varphi
pulls back as shown below:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Proof.

By fibrant replacement, we can consider families
R:B\to\mathcal{U}
,
P:F\equiv\widetilde{R}\to\mathcal{U}
,
Q:E\equiv\widetilde{P}\to\mathcal{U}
, with
F\equiv\widetilde{Q}
. The fibered equivalence
\varphi
is given by a family of equivalences

\varphi:\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:B\\
x:R\,b\end{subarray}}\big(\sum_{e:P\,b\,x}Q\,b\,x\,e\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}P\,b\,x.

The induced family

\lx@nobreakspace\varphi^{\prime}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda a,x.\varphi(k\,a,x):\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:B\\
x:R\,k(a)\end{subarray}}\big(\sum_{e:P\,k(a)\,x}Q\,k(a)\,x\,e\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}P\,k(a)\,x

also constitutes a fibered equivalence. Commutation of all the diagrams is clear since, after fibrant replacement, all the vertical maps are given by projections.
∎

Lemma 12.A.4

(Closedness of fibered equivalences under dependent products)
.

Let
I
be a type. Suppose given a family
B:I\to\mathcal{U}
and indexed families
P,Q:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}\to\mathcal{U}
together with a fiberwise equivalence
\varphi:\prod_{i:I}\prod_{b:B}P_{i}\,b\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{i}\,b
. Then the map

\lx@nobreakspace\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}:\big(\prod_{i:I}\widetilde{P_{i}}\big)\longrightarrow_{\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}\big(\prod_{i:I}\widetilde{Q_{i}}\big)

induced by taking the dependent product over
I
is a fiberwise equivalence, too.

Proof.

For
i:I
, we fibrantly replace the given fiberwise equivalence
\varphi_{i}
by projections, giving rise to (strictly) commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sum_{b:B}\sum_{x:Q_{i}(b)}P_{i}(b,x)\simeq E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}\simeq\sum_{b:B}P_{i}(b)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Now,
\varphi
being a fiberwise equivalence is equivalent to

\displaystyle\prod_{i:I}\isEquiv(\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i:I}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:B_{i}\\
x:Q_{i}(b)\end{subarray}}\isContr(P_{i}(b,x))

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{\beta:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}\prod_{\sigma:\prod_{i:I}\beta^{*}P_{i}}\prod_{i:I}\isContr\left(P_{i}(\beta(i),\sigma(i))\right).

By (weak) function extensionality,
1
1

1

cf.
[
Rij20
, Theorem 13.1.4(ii)]
, or the discussion at the beginning of
[
RS17
, Section 4.4.]
this implies

\displaystyle\prod_{\beta:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}\prod_{\sigma:\prod_{i:I}\beta^{*}Q_{i}}\isContr\left(\prod_{i:I}\langle\beta,\sigma\rangle^{*}P_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\isEquiv\left(\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}\right)

wich yields the desired statement. Note, that the latter equivalence follows by fibrant replacement of the diagram obtained by applying
\prod_{i:I}(-)
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}\\
\sigma:\prod_{i:I}\beta^{*}Q_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i:I}\langle\beta,\sigma\rangle^{*}P_{i}\simeq\prod_{i:I}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathllap{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}\simeq}\sum_{\beta:\prod_{j:I}B_{i}}\prod_{i:I}\beta^{*}Q_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i:I}\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i:I}\xi_{i}}

∎

Lemma 12.A.5

(Closedness of fibered equivalences under sliced products)
.

Given indexed families
P,Q:I\to B\to\mathcal{U}
and a family of fibered equivalences
\prod_{i:I}\prod_{b:B}P_{i}\,b\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{i}\,b
. Then the induced fibered functor

\lx@nobreakspace\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}:\prod_{i:I}\prod_{b:B}\times_{i:I}^{B}P_{i}\,b\longrightarrow\times_{i:I}^{B}Q_{i}\,b

between the sliced products over
B
is also a fibered equivalence.

Proof.

As usual, denote for
i:
by
\pi_{i}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{B}(P_{i}):E_{i}\to B
and
\xi_{i}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{B}(Q_{i}):F_{i}\to B
the unstraightenings of the given fibered families, giving rise to a (strict) diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

Since weak equivalences are closed under taking dependent products, the induced fibered map
\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}:\prod_{i:I}E_{i}\to_{I\to B}\prod_{i:I}F_{i}
is also a weak equivalence, and by right properness
Lemma

12.A.1
the desired mediating map is as well:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times^{B}_{i:I}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times^{B}_{i:I}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cst}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

∎

Proposition 12.A.6
.

For an indexing type
I
and a base Rezk type
B
, families of fibered equivalences between Rezk types over
B
are closed under taking sliced products,
i.e.
: Given a family of isoinner fibrations over
B
together with a fibered equivalence as below left, the induced maps on the right make up a fibered equivalence as well:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}G_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times_{i:I}^{B}\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

This is a consequence of
Lemmas

12.A.4
and
12.A.5
, considering the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times^{B}_{i:I}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times^{B}_{i:I}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times^{B}_{i:I}G_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}G_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

∎

Appendix 12.B
Fibered (LARI) adjunctions

Building on previous work
[
RS17
, Section 11]
and
[
BW21
, Appendix B]
we provide a characterization of fibered LARI adjunctions along similar lines.

Theorem 12.B.1

(Characterizations of fibered adjunctions, cf.
[
RS17
, Theorem 11.23]
,
[
BW21
, Theorem B.1.4]
)
.

Let
B
be a Rezk type. For
P,Q:B\to\mathcal{U}
isoinner families we write
\pi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{B}(P):E\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\widetilde{P}\twoheadrightarrow B
and
\xi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\Un_{B}(Q):F\equiv\widetilde{Q}\twoheadrightarrow B
. Given a fibered functor
\varphi:E\to_{B}F
such that (strictly)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}

the following are equivalent propositions:

1.

The type of
fibered left adjoints
of
\varphi
,
i.e.
fibered functors
\psi
which are ordinary (transposing) left adjoints of
\varphi
whose unit, moreover, is vertical.

2.

The type of fibered functors
\psi:F\to_{B}E
together with a vertical
2
-cell
\eta:\id_{F}\Rightarrow_{B}\varphi\psi
s.t.
\Phi_{\eta}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda u,d,e,k.\varphi_{u}\,k\circ\eta_{d}:\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b:B\\
u:a\to b\end{subarray}}\prod_{d:Q\,a,~e:P\,b}\big(\psi_{a}\,d\to^{P}_{u}e\big)\to\big(d\to^{Q}_{u}\varphi_{b}\,e)
is a fiberwise equivalence.

3.

The type of
sliced
(or
fiberwise
)
left adjoints (over
B
)
to
\varphi
,
i.e.
fibered functors
\psi:F\to_{B}E
together with a fibered equivalence
\psi\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}E\simeq_{F\times_{B}E}F\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}\varphi
.

4.

The type of
bi-diagrammatic fibered
(or
fiberwise
)
left adjoints
,
i.e.
fibered functors
\psi
together with:

•

a vertical natural transformation
\eta:\id_{F}\Rightarrow_{B}\varphi\psi

•

two
vertical
natural transformations
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}:\psi\varphi\Rightarrow_{B}\id_{E}

•

homotopies
2
2

2

by Segal-ness, the witnesses for the triangle identities are actually unique up to contractibility

\alpha:\varphi\varepsilon\circ\eta\varphi=_{E\to F}\id_{\varphi},~\beta:\varepsilon^{\prime}\psi\circ\psi\eta=_{F\to E}\id_{\psi}

5.

The type of fibered functors
\psi
together with:

•

a vertical natural transformation
\eta:\id_{F}\Rightarrow_{B}\varphi\psi

•

two natural transformations
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}:\psi\varphi\Rightarrow\id_{E}

•

homotopies
\alpha:\varphi\varepsilon\circ\eta\varphi=_{E\to F}\id_{\varphi},~\beta:\varepsilon^{\prime}\psi\circ\psi\eta=_{F\to E}\id_{\psi}

Proof.

At first, we prove that, given a
fixed
and
fibered
functor
\psi:F\to_{B}E
the respective witnessing data are propositions.
3
3

3

This justifies the ensuing list of
logical
equivalences.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-vert-i}\iff\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-ii}
:

This follows from the equivalence between transposing left adjoint and bi-diagrammatic left adjoint data, cf.
[
RS17
, Theorem 11.23]
.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-i}\implies\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-ii}
:

This is clear since the latter is a weakening of the former.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-ii}\implies\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-i}
:

Denoting the base component of
\varepsilon
by
v:\Delta^{1}\to(B\to B)\simeq B\to(\Delta^{1}\to B)
, projecting down from
\alpha
via
\xi
we obtain the identity
\xi\alpha:v\circ\id_{B}=\id_{B}
. Thus
\varepsilon
is vertical, and similarly one argues for
\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-sliced}\iff\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-i}
:

Given the fibered functor
\psi
, both lists of data witness that for every
b:B
the components
\psi_{b}\dashv\varphi_{b}:P\,b\to Q\,b
define an adjunction between the fibers, again by
[
RS17
, Theorem 11.23]
.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-vert-ii}\implies\ref{it:fib-ladj-sliced}
:

The latter is an instance of the former.

\ref{it:fib-ladj-bi-diag-i}\implies\ref{it:fib-ladj-vert-ii}
:

Using naturality and the triangle identities, we show that the fiberwise conditions (vertical case) can be lifted to the case of arbitrary arrows in the base.
4
4

4

I thank Ulrik Buchholtz for pointing out the subsequent argument.

Consider the transposing maps:

\displaystyle\Phi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda k.\varphi_{u}\,k\circ\eta_{d}

\displaystyle:\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b:B\\
u:a\to b\end{subarray}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}d:Q\,a\\
e:P\,b\end{subarray}}\big(\psi_{a}\,d\to^{P}_{u}e\big)\to\big(d\to^{Q}_{u}\varphi_{b}\,e\big)

\displaystyle\Psi\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda m.\varepsilon_{e}\circ\psi_{u}\,m

\displaystyle:\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b:B\\
u:a\to b\end{subarray}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}d:Q\,a\\
e:P\,b\end{subarray}}\big(d\to^{Q}_{u}\varphi_{b}\,e)\to\big(\psi_{a}\,d\to^{P}_{u}e\big)

The first roundtrip yields:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(k:\psi_{a}\,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{e\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(\varphi_{u}\,k\circ\eta_{d}:d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{b}\,e\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(\varepsilon_{e}\circ\psi_{a}(\varphi_{u}\,k\circ\eta_{d}):\psi_{a}\,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

The result yields back
k
using a triangle identity in the triangle on the left, and naturality of
\varepsilon
in the square on the right:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\psi_{a}\,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\psi\varphi\psi)_{a}\,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\psi\varphi)_{b}\,e}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\psi_{a}\,d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{a}\eta_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\id_{\psi_{a}\,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{a}\varepsilon_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\psi\varphi)_{u}\,k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varepsilon_{e}}

In addition, we have also used naturality of
\varepsilon
for
\psi_{a}\varepsilon_{d}\equiv\varepsilon_{\psi_{a}\,d}
. An analogous argument proves the other roundtrip.

We have proven, that relative to a fixed fibered functor
\psi:F\to_{B}E
the different kinds of witnesses that this is a fibered left adjoint to
\varphi
are equivalent propositions, giving rise to a predicate
{\mathrm{isFibLAdj}}_{\varphi}:(F\to_{B}E)\to{\mathrm{Prop}}
. What about the
\Sigma
-type
{\mathrm{FibLAdj}}_{\varphi}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\sum_{\psi:F\to_{B}E}{\mathrm{isFibLAdj}}_{\varphi}(\psi)
as a whole? E.g. using the data from
item

3
(after conversion via
[
RS17
, Theorem 11.23]
), said type is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\mathrm{FibLAdj}}_{\varphi}(\psi)

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{\psi:\prod_{b:B}P\,b\to Q\,b}\sum_{\eta:\prod_{b:B}\prod_{d:Q\,b}\hom_{Q\,b}(d,(\varphi\,\psi)_{b}\,d)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:B\\
d:Q\,b\\
e:P\,b\end{subarray}}\isEquiv\big(\lambda k.\varphi_{b}(k)\circ\eta_{d}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}b:B\\
d:Q\,b\end{subarray}}\sum_{\psi_{b}:P\,b}\sum_{\eta_{d}:d\to_{Q\,b}\varphi_{b}(\psi_{b}d)}\prod_{e:P\,b}\isEquiv(\lambda k.\varphi_{b}(k)\circ\eta_{d}).

Finally, one shows that this is indeed a proposition, completely analogously to the argument given in the proof of
[
RS17
, Theorem 11.23]
for the non-dependent case.
∎

Definition 12.B.2

(Fibered (LARI) adjunction)
.

Let
B
be a Rezk type and
\pi:E\twoheadrightarrow B
,
\xi:F\twoheadrightarrow B
be isoinner fibrations, with
P\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\St_{B}(\pi)
and
Q\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\St_{B}(\xi)
. Given a fibered functor
\varphi:E\to_{B}F
, the data of a
fibered left adjoint right inverse (LARI) adjunction
is given by

•

a fibered functor
\psi:F\to_{B}E
,

•

and an equivalence
\Phi:\psi\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}E\simeq_{F\times_{B}E}F\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}\varphi
s.t. the fibered unit

\lx@nobreakspace\eta_{\Phi}\mathrel{\vcentcolon\equiv}\lambda b,d.\Phi_{b,d,\psi_{b}\,d}(\id_{\psi_{b}\,d}):\prod_{b:B}\prod_{d:Q\,b}d\to_{Q\,b}(\varphi\psi)_{b}(d)

is a componentwise homotopy.

Together, this defines the data of a
fibered LARI adjunction
. Diagrammatically, we represent this by:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

In fact, as established in the previous works of
[
RS17
, Section 11]
the unit of a coherent adjunction is determined uniquely up to homotopy. Hence, using the characterizations of a (coherent) LARI adjunction, the type of fibered LARI adjunctions in the above sense is equivalent to the type of LARI adjunctions which are also fibered adjunctions. This implies the validity of the familiar closure properties for this restricted class as well.

Appendix 12.C
Sliced commas and products

We record here explicitly some closure properties involving sliced commas and products that are often used, especially in the treatise of two-sided fibrations and related notions.

Proposition 12.C.1

(Dependent products of sliced commas)
.

For a type
I
and
i:I
, given fibered cospans

\lx@nobreakspace\psi_{i}:F_{i}\to_{B_{i}}G_{i}\leftarrow_{B_{i}}E_{i}:\varphi

of Rezk types, taking the dependent product fiberwisely commutes with forming sliced comma types:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B_{i}}\varphi_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}(\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B_{i}}\varphi_{i})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\prod_{i:I}\psi_{i}\right)\downarrow_{\left(\prod_{i:I}B_{i}\right)}\left(\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proof.

We denote by
P_{i},Q_{i},R_{i}:B_{i}\to\mathcal{U}
the straightenings of the given maps
E_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}
,
F_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}
, and
G_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B_{i}
, resp.
Using fibrant replacement, the sliced commas are computed as

\displaystyle\psi_{i}\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B_{i}}\varphi_{i}\simeq\sum_{b:B_{i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e:P_{i}(b)\\
d:Q_{i}(b)\end{subarray}}\big(\psi_{i}\big)_{b}(d)\longrightarrow_{R_{i}(b)}\big(\varphi_{i}\big)_{b}(e).

(1)

From this and the type-theoretic axiom of choice, we obtain as fibrant replacement for

\prod_{i:I}(\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B_{i}}\varphi_{i})\twoheadrightarrow\prod_{i:I}B_{i}

the type

\displaystyle\prod_{i:I}(\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B_{i}}\varphi_{i})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{eq:fam-sliced-comma})}}}{{\simeq}}\prod_{i:I}\sum_{b:B_{i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e:P_{i}(b)\\
d:Q_{i}(b)\end{subarray}}\big(\psi_{i}\big)_{b}(d)\longrightarrow_{R_{i}(b)}\big(\varphi_{i}\big)_{b}(e)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(AC)}}}{{\simeq}}\sum_{\beta:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}\prod_{i:I}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}e:P_{i}(\beta(i))\\
d:Q_{i}(\beta(i))\end{subarray}}\big(\psi_{i}\big)_{\beta(i)}(d)\longrightarrow_{R_{i}(\beta(i))}\big(\varphi_{i}\big)_{\beta(i)}(e)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(\ref{eq:fam-sliced-comma})}}}{{\simeq}}\sum_{\beta:\prod_{i:I}B_{i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma:\prod_{i:I}P_{i}(\beta(i))\\
\tau:\prod_{i:I}Q_{i}(\beta(i))\end{subarray}}\big(\prod_{i:I}\psi(\tau)\big)\to_{\big(\prod_{i:I}R_{i}(\beta(i))\big)}\big(\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}(\sigma)\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(AC)}}}{{\simeq}}\left(\prod_{i:I}\psi_{i}\right)\downarrow_{\left(\prod_{i:I}B_{i}\right)}\left(\prod_{i:I}\varphi_{i}\right)

This yields the desired fibered equivalence.
∎

Corollary 12.C.2

(Products of commas in a slice)
.

Fix a base Rezk type
B
be and an indexing type
I
. Given for
i:I
an isoinner fibration
\pi_{i}:E_{i}\twoheadrightarrow B
consider a cospan of isoinner fibrations
\psi_{i}:F_{i}\to E_{i}\leftarrow G_{i}:\varphi
. Then we have a fibered equivalence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}\big(\psi_{i}\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}\varphi_{i}\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}\right)\mathbin{\downarrow}_{B}\left(\times_{i:I}^{B}\psi_{i}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}\times_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\prod_{i:I}F_{i}\right)\times_{I\to B}\left(\prod_{i:I}E_{i}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Proposition 12.C.3

(Fibered (LARI) adjunctions are preserved by sliced products)
.

For an indexing type
I
and a base Rezk type
B
, families of fibered (LARI) adjunctions between Rezk types over
B
are closed under taking sliced products,
i.e.
: Given a family of isoinner fibrations over
B
together with a fibered (LARI) adjunction as below left, the
induced maps on the right make up a fibered (LARI) adjunction as well:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times_{i:I}^{B}\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

Given a family of fibered adjunctions as indicated amounts to a family of fibered equivalences, themselves fibered over
B
, for
i:I
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varphi_{i}\downarrow_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{i}\times_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Taking the dependent product over
i:I
produces a fibered equivalence, itself fibered over
B^{I}
. Pullback along
\mathrm{cst}:B\to B^{I}
yields the sliced products and again preserves the fibered equivalence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\times_{i:I}^{B}\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B}E_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}\psi_{i}\downarrow_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}\downarrow_{B}\varphi_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}\downarrow_{B}\varphi_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}\times_{B}E_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i:I}F_{i}\times_{B}E_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{cst}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Since sliced products canonically commute with both sliced commas and fiber products, this gives a fibered equivalence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}\psi_{i}\big)\downarrow_{I\to B}\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}E_{i}\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}\big)\downarrow_{I\to B}\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}\varphi_{i}\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}F_{i}\big)\times_{I\to B}\big(\times_{i:I}^{B}E_{i}\big)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

which exactly yields the desired fibered adjunction of the sliced products.
∎
